## 6. Verification approach

Every auto-marked item has two independent implementations of its answer (content-format.md 6.2): the TypeScript generator (or the author's declared answer) that builds the item, and a Python verifier written from the generator's plain-language `semantics` without reading the TypeScript, run in CI under `tools/zqverify` with pinned SymPy, NumPy, SciPy and mpmath. CI also replays every listed distractor through the runtime grader and requires it to be marked wrong with its intended diagnosis. Draft 2 ran these checks by hand on every number the file states, in three scripts written for it: 49 checks on the new hooks, edges and the sample lesson; 66 re-running the draft 1 numbers it keeps, which found lesson 32's compounding convention; and 175 running a reference solution and every known-wrong solution of the 25 homework and 8 practice drills against the tests section 3 states. They ran on Python 3.11.15 with SymPy 1.14.0, NumPy 2.4.6, SciPy 1.17.1 and mpmath 1.3.0, and `tools/zqverify`'s 1.3 suite must reproduce them. Calculus adds one rule of its own: **where two methods exist, the second implementation uses the other method** (shells against discs, polar against Cartesian, the swapped order of integration, substitution into the differential equation against `dsolve`), because a shared method would share its mistakes.

| Family | Items | CI: second implementation | Runtime check | Traps the check must catch |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Limits | D1, U1 (lesson 34), X3 (lesson 103), M1 (lesson 108) | SymPy `limit` from each side (`dir='+'`, `dir='-'`), compared; mpmath at 50 digits along $h = 10^{-k}$, $k = 1$ to 12, as a sanity check; two-variable limits along five seeded paths including a parabola | `numeric` with the specials "does not exist", $+\infty$ and $-\infty$ | One-sided limits that disagree; a misleading table ($\sin\frac{\pi}{x}$ at $x = \frac1n$); a path limit that differs only along a curve |
| $\varepsilon$-$\delta$ | D1 (lessons 4, 5), homework D1 | The generator's $\delta(\varepsilon)$ checked by mpmath interval arithmetic on $0 < \lvert x - a \rvert < \delta$ for 50 log-spaced $\varepsilon$ in $[10^{-6}, 1]$ | `predicate: delta-works` (widgets-calc-linalg.md 5.2) and `delta-formula-works`; a typed $\delta$ may contain only $\varepsilon$, so a $\delta$ depending on $x$ is rejected at parse time with its diagnosis | A $\delta$ that works for large $\varepsilon$ and fails for small; a $\delta$ that uses $x$ |
| Derivatives | D2 to D5 | SymPy `diff`; `simplify(answer - key) == 0`, plus probing at 8 seeded points inside the declared domain; implicit derivatives by `idiff`; parametric by the quotient of $t$-derivatives | `expression` equivalence (content-format.md 7.3) under the item's `vars` domains, with forms where the item asks for one ("in terms of $x$ only") | $\ln\lvert x \rvert$ against $\ln x$ (probes include negative $x$ when the domain does); a missing chain factor; sign slips on $\cos$; an answer correct only at the sampled points (8 probes make that negligible) |
| Rates and approximations | D4 (lesson 24), U1 | SymPy with time-dependent functions, differentiated before substitution; Newton and fixed-point iterates in exact fractions or mpmath at 50 digits, with the iterate index declared | `numeric` with a relative tolerance of $10^{-4}$, or exact where the item says so | Substituting the instant's values before differentiating (every rate becomes 0); a cycling Newton start, detected by repeated iterates |
| Sign charts, extrema and sketches | U2, U3 | SymPy `solveset` over the reals for $f' = 0$ and $f'' = 0$; signs tested at exact midpoints; features (critical points, inflections, asymptotes, intercepts) listed and compared with a 10,000-point mpmath scan | `set` and `interval` for sign charts; `curve` with a band, coverage and features for sketches (content-format.md 7.5) | A double root of $f'$ classified as a turning point; an inflection reported where $f'' = 0$ without a sign change |
| Optimisation | U4, M3 | SymPy solves the first-order conditions; second-order or Hessian test; boundary candidates evaluated; the global optimum confirmed by a dense grid (10,000 points in one variable, 400 by 400 in two); Kelly and portfolio items cross-checked by a seeded simulation of $10^5$ steps | `numeric`, `point`, `set` (order-free lists of points with type labels) | A root of $f' = 0$ outside the domain; a local optimum reported as global; a boundary maximum missed |
| Convexity and Jensen | U5 | $f'' \ge 0$ on the domain by `solveset(f'' < 0)` being empty; $10^4$ random chords tested; expectations in exact fractions | `choice`, `numeric` | "$f'' > 0$" items where $f''$ vanishes at a point; an expectation computed as $f(E[X])$ |
| Riemann sums and numerical rules | I1, I4 (lesson 75) | Exact `Fraction` sums for polynomial integrands, mpmath at 50 digits otherwise; error ratios computed, never assumed | `numeric` exact or with a stated tolerance; code drills by tests | Left and right swapped; Simpson with odd $n$; a stated error ratio that the integrand's smoothness does not support |
| Integrals and antiderivatives | I2, I3 | SymPy `integrate`; the antiderivative differentiated back and compared with the integrand by `simplify` and probing; definite values against mpmath `quad` at 30 digits; SymPy `singularities` on the interval, and generators reject intervals containing one unless the item is about improper integrals | `expression` with `relation: antiderivative` (the learner's answer differentiated and compared with the integrand by probing, so any constant passes) | $\ln x$ for $\ln\lvert x \rvert$; a definite integral taken across a singularity; $F(a) - F(b)$ |
| Improper integrals and convergence | I4 | SymPy `integrate` to $\infty$; the verdict cross-checked by mpmath `quad` with growing limits (values settle to 10 digits, or grow without bound) and by the authored comparison function; disagreement fails CI | `choice` for the verdict and `numeric` for the value, as a multi-part item | $\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x\,dx$ called 0; a vertical asymptote inside the range |
| Densities and expectations | I4, M6 (lesson 135) | Exact SymPy integrals; every generated density asserted to integrate to 1 and to be non-negative; a seeded Monte Carlo of $10^6$ draws as a third check, within 4 standard errors | `numeric` with the $[0, 1]$ nudge for probabilities | A density value read as a probability; $E[X^2]$ taken as $(E[X])^2$ |
| Areas, volumes, arc length, polar area, work | I5 | The other method: shells against discs, horizontal strips against vertical, Cartesian against polar, both required to agree in SymPy; arc lengths without closed form by mpmath `quad` | `numeric` with exact forms accepted | A washer computed as $\pi(R - r)^2$; the polar $\frac12$ missing; a shell radius from the wrong axis |
| Differential equations | I6 | SymPy `dsolve` and `checkodesol`; equilibria by `solve` and stability by the sign of the right-hand side's derivative; Euler steps by an exact `Fraction` recursion | `expression` for solutions (substituted into the equation by probing in $t$, with the initial condition checked separately); `numeric` for values | A lost equilibrium solution; $e^{kt} + C$ in place of $Ce^{kt}$ |
| Series: convergence, sums, radii | X1, X2 | The authored test with its parameters (such as "ratio limit $\frac12$") verified by SymPy `limit`, plus SymPy `Sum(...).is_convergent()`; where SymPy returns no verdict, mpmath `nsum` with acceleration and an owner-reviewed proof sketch, flagged as checked by review rather than machine; radii from both the ratio and the root of the coefficients; each endpoint checked separately; rearrangement items by exact partial sums to $10^6$ terms | `choice` for verdicts, `interval` for intervals of convergence, `numeric` for sums | Endpoints decided by the ratio test; "terms go to 0, so it converges"; a formula used outside its radius |
| Taylor | X3, U1 (lesson 32) | SymPy `series` to the stated order with exact coefficients; for remainder items, the true error by mpmath must not exceed the bound, and the stated number of terms must be the least that meets it (one fewer must fail); finance expansions against exact repricing | `expression` with a structural check that no term beyond the stated order is present; `numeric` for bounds | Missing $\frac{1}{k!}$; powers of $x$ where $(x - a)$ is needed; a bound evaluated at the centre |
| Partial derivatives, gradients, chain rule | M1, M2 | SymPy `diff`; directional derivatives with the direction normalised by the verifier, whatever the item supplies; constrained partials by explicit substitution of the held variable; backpropagation items against SymPy's gradient of the composed loss and a numerical gradient check | `expression` per component; `numeric` | A non-unit direction; the held variable confused; gradients not summed where a variable feeds two nodes |
| Critical points and Hessians | M3 | SymPy `solve` and `nonlinsolve` on $\nabla f = \mathbf 0$ (both must return the same real set); classification by the signs of the Hessian's eigenvalues; items with $D = 0$ are authored, never generated | `set` of points with type labels, order-free | Solutions lost by dividing by a variable; $D < 0$ classified by $f_{xx}$ |
| Lagrange multipliers | M4 | SymPy solves the Lagrange system and checks for points where $\nabla g = \mathbf 0$ on the constraint; an independent dense scan along a parametrisation of the constraint must find the same optimum to $10^{-9}$; the multiplier is checked against a central difference of the optimal value in the constraint level $c$ | `numeric`, `point`, `expression` | A missing candidate; the multiplier's sign under a different writing of the constraint (each item states its convention) |
| Multiple integrals and the Jacobian | M5, M6 | SymPy in the stated order and in the swapped order or other coordinates (both must agree); SciPy `nquad` as a numeric third check; Jacobians by SymPy `Matrix.jacobian(...).det()` | Bounds graded as a region, not as text: the learner's bounds and the key's must give the same integral of 1 and of two seeded probe functions, so any correct description passes; values by `numeric` | The missing $r$ or $\rho^2\sin\phi$; limits that depend on a variable already integrated out; an order swapped without redrawing |
| Long-form items | every set and the finals | CI checks the model answer's algebraic lines with SymPy (each line equivalent to the last under the stated assumptions) and that each rubric point names model lines that exist (content-format.md 6.8) | Self-marking against the rubric with Bertie's suggested mark and evidence quote beside it (ai-tutor.md 2.7); the learner's mark counts | Rubric points no line of the model answer evidences; a model line that silently divides by a quantity that can be 0 (the verifier checks each division's domain) |
| Code drills | every set, the practice drills, Calculus Lab | `zqtest` suites run against `reference.py` on CPython 3.11 and on Pyodide's CPython 3.14 (`content-format.md` 6.11); every `wrong/*.py` must fail the test it names; each drill's `needs` is checked against its imports, so a `needs: []` drill that imports NumPy fails; seeded tests are replayed on both runtimes and must agree within each test's tolerance (built-in `sum` differs in its last digits between 3.11 and 3.14); Calculus Lab tests run on CPython with NumPy and SciPy | Visible examples on Run; hidden tests at submission; the first failing test mapped to a diagnosis | Floating-point comparisons (`math.isclose` everywhere); infinite loops in Newton and bisection (per-test time limits); `math.exp` overflow |
| Companion hours | every level | CI sums each level's `companion` hours, checks every `src` id against `content/sources.yaml`, and checks the topic's content estimates against 200 h within 10% (`content-format.md` 3.8, section 1.4) | None | A level whose rows do not reach its stated total; an id with no source entry; an OpenStax row marked `problem-adapted` (the licence forbids it) |
| Tutor fields | every task screen | The compiler extracts every number, fraction and expression from `tutor.focus`, `tutor.notes` and `tutor.avoid` and grades it with the screen's own check; any that grades correct fails the build (`content-format.md` 3.10) | The leak probe on Bertie's replies (`ai-tutor.md` 8.1) | A lesson-wide note that names the screen's answer (draft 1's lesson 1 note named 20, the limit on screen 5) |
| The finals paper | `exm.calc.finals` | CI rules of assessment.md 3.2 on every form: equal marks, the 20/50/20/10 mix within 5 points, at least 80% of levels covered, at least 50% of marks from generators, reserve capacity for six fresh sittings; the Section A bar unit-tested on synthetic results (69/100 with 50/70 fails; 70/100 with 48/70 fails; 70/100 with 49/70 passes) | Exam engine (assessment.md 3) | A form that drifts off the mix; a pass that ignores the section bar |

**What verification cannot prove** (content-format.md 6.13): that a hook is engaging, that a trap catches the misconception it targets, that a long-form rubric rewards understanding rather than vocabulary, or that a convergence verdict SymPy cannot decide is right. The last is reviewed by the owner and flagged in the item's metadata; the rest are checked by the owner's review of each level before release and by item statistics afterwards: a trap screen that fewer than 30% of learners fall into, or a distractor nobody picks, is flagged for rewriting.

---

## 7. Sample lesson in full: "Speed at an instant"

**Why this lesson.** Decision 13: every later lesson in 1.3 rests on the difference quotient and its limit. 0.2 lesson 109 has already estimated a gradient from shrinking chords, met the $\frac00$ trap and the corner of $\lvert x \rvert$, and 0.3 level R5 has made limits respectable. What is left, and where 1.3 begins, is the rate at an instant found exactly, the exact error of any one window, the rate as a function of time, and the two facts that stop a learner over-trusting the picture: a centred window is better but not exact, and a rate of zero is not rest. Draft 1's version of this lesson repeated 0.2 lesson 109's hook, trap and edge; this one does not.

### 7.1 Lesson header

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Id | `lsn.deriv.instant-speed` (course D, level D1, lesson 1 of 1.3) |
| Length | 10 screens (1 title, 8 tasks, 1 summary) and a quiz of 5 one-mark items; `est: { minutes: 15 }`, about 11 minutes of screens and 4 of quiz |
| Requires | 0.2 lessons 43 (distance-time graphs) and 109 (gradient from shrinking chords); 0.3 lessons 142 (approaching a value) and 144 (limit laws and algebraic techniques); 0.2's expansion of brackets; skill ids as the Stage 0 files name them |
| Introduces | `skl.calc.difference-quotient`, `skl.calc.instantaneous-rate`, `skl.calc.rate-function`, `skl.calc.window-error` |
| Terms | `gls.calc.average-rate`, `gls.calc.difference-quotient`, `gls.calc.instantaneous-rate`; embargoed for Bertie until screen 5 (content-format.md 3.10). "Derivative", "tangent", "differentiate" and $f'$ are embargoed for the whole lesson (lessons 8 and 10 name them) |
| Frame (`frame.why`) | "A radar gun, a speed camera and a bank's risk system all report a rate, and each one measures over a window: of time, or of price. An estimate is not enough when a fine, a trade or a hedge depends on it. We need the rate at the instant itself, exactly, and the exact size of a window's error." |
| Before (`frame.before`) | "You have estimated a gradient by sliding a chord towards a point (0.2 lesson 109) and evaluated limits by cancelling (0.3 lesson 144). Today the two meet." |
| Insight (the aha line) | The rate at an instant is what the average rates approach as the window shrinks; simplifying while the window is open finds it exactly, and the term left over is exactly the window's error |
| Misconceptions | `mc.calc.position-as-rate`, `mc.calc.change-not-rate`, `mc.calc.average-from-start`, `mc.calc.average-equals-instant`, `mc.calc.instant-undefined`, `mc.calc.negative-window-negative-rate`, `mc.calc.expand-square-wrong`, `mc.calc.substitute-before-simplify`, `mc.calc.reading-as-error`, `mc.calc.error-is-window`, `mc.calc.centred-window-exact`, `mc.calc.centred-same-as-one-sided`, `mc.calc.instant-is-zero`, `mc.calc.zero-rate-means-stopped`, `mc.calc.turnaround-no-rate`, `mc.calc.limit-is-last-value` |
| Edge cases (`edgeCases`) | A window that ends at the instant, $h < 0$ (at `shrink`); a rate of 0 at the top of a throw, which is not rest (at `top`); the centred window, exact at every instant for every polynomial of degree at most 2 and for no other polynomial (at `centred`, then quiz Q4) |
| Worked example with a decision point (`workedExample`) | At `exact`. Naive: put $h = 0$ into $\frac{5(2 + h)^2 - 20}{h}$ and get $\frac00$. Decision: rewrite while $h \ne 0$ as $20 + 5h$; then $h \to 0$ gives 20, and the $5h$ that remains for any open window is the radar's error |
| Trap | `centred`: the cart at $x(t) = t^3$, bait "the centred radar reads exactly 3" (`mc.calc.centred-window-exact`) |
| Transfer | `delta`: a contract paying $x^2$, its bump-and-reprice reading and its delta |
| Sources | `src.openstax-calculus`, vol. 1 §2.1 (A Preview of Calculus) and §3.1 (Defining the Derivative), `basis`; `src.mit-1801-2006`, Lecture 1, `parallel-reading`; `src.3b1b-calculus`, chapter 2 (The paradox of the derivative), `parallel-reading`; all `checked: false` [check] |
| Art | `ast.course-art.deriv-radar` (a falling ball, a radar cone and a stopwatch); course art, not Bertie, who appears only in his own slots (bertie.md 7) |
| Lesson-wide tutor guidance | Avoid "derivative", "differentiate", "tangent" and "slope at a point"; never state a speed or a rate on a screen before the learner's first Check there; prefer questions about windows ("what does a smaller window read?") to statements |

**The widgets.** Screens 2 to 9 use `secant-tangent` (content id `scene`) in a **motion preset** this lesson asks the catalogue to add (section 7.5): on the left, a tower with a ruler in metres (`ruler:tower`) and a ball animated from the position function, with Play and a time scrubber in steps of 0.1 s; on the right, the graph of the position function (`curve:s`) with handle $P$ at the instant ($t = a$, `handle:P`) and handle $Q$ at $t = a + h$ (`handle:Q`), the secant through them (`line:secant`), braces labelled "time taken" (`brace:run`) and "distance fallen" (`brace:rise`), and a readout that shows the secant's slope as a radar reading (`readout:radar`). The window slider (`slider:h`) is signed, with stops that never include 0. A **centred-window mode** adds a second handle at $a - h$ (`handle:Qm`). When a screen needs a table, a `value-table` (content id `table`) sits below, with rows ordered by $h$ from most negative to most positive and cells `cell:<row>,<column>`, rows counted from 0 (so on screen 3 row 2 is $h = -0.01$ and row 5 is $h = 0.01$); the dashed line the secants settle on is `line:tangent`, an internal id the learner never sees named. A `derivation-steps` widget has content id `d`.

### 7.2 Screens

---

**Screen 1. Title** (`title`, id `title`)

- **Prose:** "A radar gun, a speed camera and a risk system all measure a rate over a window. Today: the rate at an instant, with no window at all, and exactly how wrong a window is."
- **Art:** `ast.course-art.deriv-radar`.

---

**Screen 2. The hook** (`problem`, id `hook`, two parts; opens as free manipulation, and part (a) appears once the learner has played the drop: `continueWhen: scene.played >= 1`)

- **Prose:** "A ball is dropped from a tall tower. After $t$ seconds it has fallen $s(t) = 5t^2$ metres. A radar gun measures how far the ball falls during a short window of time and divides by the window's length."
- **Widget:** `scene`, motion preset, $s(t) = 5t^2$ for $0 \le t \le 4$. $P$ is locked at $t = 2$ and $Q$ at $t = 2.1$; the secant and both braces are drawn; the brace values and the radar readout stay hidden until part (a) is checked.
- **Part (a) question:** "The radar's window runs from $t = 2$ to $t = 2.1$. What does it report?"
- **Answer input:** `numeric-input`, unit "m/s".
- **Correct:** 20.5. **Reason:** "The ball falls $22.05 - 20 = 2.05$ m in 0.1 s."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| 22.05 | `mc.calc.position-as-rate` | "22.05 m is how far the ball has fallen by $t = 2.1$: a position. The radar divides the distance fallen during the window by the window's length." |
| 2.05 | `mc.calc.change-not-rate` | "2.05 m is the distance fallen during the window. Divide it by the 0.1 s it took." |
| 10.5 | `mc.calc.average-from-start` | "$22.05 \div 2.1 = 10.5$ is the average since the ball was dropped. The radar only watches the window from 2 to 2.1." |
| 205 | slip | "Check the division: $2.05 \div 0.1 = 20.5$." |
| `otherwise` | | "Find $s(2)$ and $s(2.1)$, subtract, and divide by the 0.1 s between them." |

- **Part (b) question:** "Is the ball's speed at the instant $t = 2$ higher than the reading, lower, or the same?" (`choice`)

| Option | Correct? | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lower: the ball speeds up during the window, so the average includes faster moments | yes | | "Yes. The ball gets faster all the way down, so the window's later moments pull the average above the speed at $t = 2$." |
| Higher | no | slip | "The ball is speeding up, so at the start of the window it is slower than on average, not faster." |
| The same: an average speed is the speed | no | `mc.calc.average-equals-instant` | "An average over a window equals the speed at its start only if the speed never changes. This ball speeds up all the time." |
| It cannot be known without a smaller window | no | `mc.calc.instant-undefined` | "It can: the ball speeds up throughout the window, so the start is slower than the average. How much slower is the question of this lesson." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "How far does the ball fall between $t = 2$ and $t = 2.1$, and how long does that take?" Point: "These two braces are what the radar divides", highlighting `scene/brace:rise` and `scene/brace:run`. Step: "$s(2) = 5 \times 4 = 20$ m and $s(2.1) = 5 \times 4.41 = 22.05$ m: 2.05 m in 0.1 s."
- **Why?** (`rep: picture`) Yours: "$\frac{22.05 - 20}{0.1} = 20.5$ m/s: the slope of the secant from $P$ to $Q$." Then: "That is an average over the window. The ball speeds up all the way down, so at $t = 2$ itself it is slower; by exactly how much is what the next screens find."
- **Solution line:** "(a) $\frac{s(2.1) - s(2)}{0.1} = \frac{2.05}{0.1} = 20.5$ m/s. (b) Lower, because the speed rises through the window."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "A radar reading is an average over a window: change in distance over change in time." Notes: "A learner who types 22.05 has read a position; ask what the radar divides. Part (b) is a prediction: accept any sound reasoning about speeding up." Reps: `picture`, `algebra`. Avoid: "derivative", "limit".

---

**Screen 3. Shrinking windows, from both sides** (`ladder`, id `shrink`; widget `from: hook/scene`, with a `value-table`)

- **Constraint:** "Shrink the window, but never to zero. A window may also end at $t = 2$."
- **Widget:** the screen 2 scene with $Q$ now driven by `slider:h`, stops at $h = -1, -0.1, -0.01, -0.001, 0.001, 0.01, 0.1, 1$; for $h < 0$, $Q$ sits to the left of $P$. The `table` has columns $h$, $s(2 + h)$, distance fallen and reading, and fills one row each time the learner visits a stop. The reading cells for $h = 0.01$ (row 5) and $h = -0.01$ (row 2) stay blank for the learner. The secant swings as $h$ changes.
- **Question:** "Fill in the radar readings for $h = 0.01$ and $h = -0.01$."
- **Answer input:** two `numeric-input` cells in the table.
- **Correct:** 20.05 and 19.95. **Reason:** "$\frac{0.2005}{0.01} = 20.05$ and $\frac{-0.1995}{-0.01} = 19.95$."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| 0.2005 or 0.1995 | `mc.calc.change-not-rate` | "That is the distance fallen. Divide it by $h$." |
| 20.2005 or 19.8005 | `mc.calc.position-as-rate` | "That is where the ball is at $t = 2 + h$. Subtract the 20 m fallen by $t = 2$, then divide by $h$." |
| $-19.95$ | `mc.calc.negative-window-negative-rate` | "For a window ending at $t = 2$ the distance and the time are both negative, $\frac{19.8005 - 20}{-0.01}$, and the signs cancel." |
| 2,005 or 1,995 | slip | "Check the division by 0.01." |
| `otherwise` | | "The reading is $\frac{s(2 + h) - s(2)}{h}$; for $h = -0.01$ the window runs from 1.99 to 2." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "What is $s(2.01)$? And $s(1.99)$?" Point: "Fill these two cells", highlighting `table/cell:5,reading` and `table/cell:2,reading`. Step: "$s(2.01) = 5 \times 4.0401 = 20.2005$ and $s(1.99) = 5 \times 3.9601 = 19.8005$. Subtract 20 from each and divide by $h$."
- **Why?** (`rep: count`) Yours: "From the right the readings run 20.5, 20.05, 20.005; from the left 19.5, 19.95, 19.995. Each tenfold shrink takes both sides ten times closer to one number." Then: "0.2 lesson 109 stopped here, with a number the table suggests. The next screen proves it and measures the error exactly."
- **Solution line:** "$\frac{20.2005 - 20}{0.01} = 20.05$; $\frac{19.8005 - 20}{-0.01} = 19.95$."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "Readings from windows on both sides of the instant close in on one number." Notes: "A learner who gives a negative reading has lost the sign of $h$; ask how long a window from 1.99 to 2 lasts and in which direction it runs." Reps: `count`, `picture`. Avoid: naming the number the readings approach before Check.

---

**Screen 4. Making it exact** (`derive`, id `exact`; the lesson's worked example with a decision point)

- **Leap** (`leap`). Naive: put $h = 0$ into $\frac{5(2 + h)^2 - 20}{h}$. Fails: it gives $\frac00$, as 0.2 lesson 109 showed, because the reading divides by the very window being closed. Points to: while $h \ne 0$, the quotient can be rewritten with no $h$ in a denominator, and that form can be evaluated at $h = 0$.
- **Prose:** "The table suggests a number. Algebra can prove it, and tell us exactly how far every reading is from it. Rewrite the reading while the window is still open."
- **Widget:** `derivation-steps` (`d`), fill mode, each line with its reason in the margin; line 4 links to the reading column of screen 3's table.
  1. $\frac{s(2 + h) - s(2)}{h} = \frac{5(2 + h)^2 - 20}{h}$ (the reading for a window of length $h$)
  2. $= \frac{5(\textbf{[slot A]}) - 20}{h}$ (expand the square)
  3. $= \frac{20h + 5h^2}{h}$ (multiply out; the 20s cancel)
  4. $= \textbf{[slot B]}$, for $h \ne 0$ (divide top and bottom by $h$, allowed because $h \ne 0$)
  5. $\to 20$ as $h \to 0$ (nothing divides by $h$ any more)
- **Question:** "Complete lines 2 and 4."
- **Answer input:** two `expression-input` slots; slot A requires the expanded form.
- **Correct:** slot A $4 + 4h + h^2$ (terms in any order); slot B $20 + 5h$. **Reason:** "For every open window the reading is exactly $20 + 5h$."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| Slot A $4 + h^2$ | `mc.calc.expand-square-wrong` | "$(2 + h)^2$ is not $4 + h^2$: the cross term $2 \times h$ appears twice." |
| Slot A $4 + 2h + h^2$ | slip | "The cross term appears twice: $2h + 2h = 4h$." |
| Slot A $(2 + h)^2$ | (form) | "Equal, but not yet expanded. Line 3 needs the separate terms." |
| Slot B $20h + 5h^2$ | slip | "Now divide each term by $h$." |
| Slot B $20$ | `mc.calc.substitute-before-simplify` | "Keep the $5h$ in this line: it is the gap of 0.05 and 0.005 in your table. It disappears only in line 5, as $h$ shrinks." |
| Slot B $20 + 5h^2$ or $20 + h$ | slip | "$\frac{5h^2}{h} = 5h$." |
| `otherwise` | | "Expand the square term by term, then divide every term of line 3 by $h$." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "Write $(2 + h)^2$ as $(2 + h)(2 + h)$: four products." Point: "Start with this line", highlighting `d/line:2`. Step: "$(2 + h)(2 + h) = 4 + 2h + 2h + h^2$; times 5, minus 20, gives line 3, and dividing by $h$ gives line 4."
- **Why?** (`rep: algebra`) Yours: "For every window $h \ne 0$ the reading is exactly $20 + 5h$, and as $h \to 0$ that goes to 20." Then: "Cancelling $h$ was allowed only because $h \ne 0$, and $h \ne 0$ is exactly the case a limit looks at. The $5h$ left over is the radar's error, to the last digit."
- **Solution line:** "$\frac{5(4 + 4h + h^2) - 20}{h} = \frac{20h + 5h^2}{h} = 20 + 5h \to 20$."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "Simplify the reading while the window is open; the leftover term is the window's error." Notes: "A learner who asks whether dividing by $h$ is illegal has the right worry: point to the condition on line 4. L'Hôpital's rule waits for lesson 34; do not mention it." Reps: `algebra`, `picture`. Avoid: "L'Hôpital", "derivative".

The decision point is the leap: substitution is the method that served in 0.3 lesson 144 whenever the function was continuous, and here it fails because the quotient is undefined at the very point the question is about. The rewrite costs one line and buys both the answer and its error.

---

**Screen 5. The names** (`generalise`, id `name`)

- **Prose:** "You have worked with two kinds of rate. Over a window from $t = 2$ to $t = 2 + h$: the **average rate of change** $\frac{s(2 + h) - s(2)}{h}$, also called a **difference quotient**. At the instant itself: the **instantaneous rate of change**, the number the averages approach as $h \to 0$ from either side, with $h$ never equal to 0."
- **Rule** (`rule`, `tex`): $\text{rate at } t = 2 \;=\; \lim_{h \to 0}\frac{s(2 + h) - s(2)}{h} \;=\; \lim_{h \to 0}(20 + 5h) \;=\; 20$, and a window of length $h$ reads $20 + 5h$, so its error is exactly $5h$. The rule describes what the learner did on screens 3 and 4; its numbers are their own.
- **Widget:** the graph alone; the tower shrinks to a thumbnail (concreteness fading). As $h$ shrinks the secant swings towards a dashed line through $P$, which appears only after Check.
- **Question:** "The radar's window is 0.1 s. By exactly how much does its reading exceed the speed at $t = 2$? And with a window of 0.004 s?"
- **Answer input:** two `numeric-input` fields, unit "m/s".
- **Correct:** 0.5 and 0.02. **Reason:** "The error is $5h$: $5 \times 0.1$ and $5 \times 0.004$."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| First field 20.5, or second 20.02 | `mc.calc.reading-as-error` | "That is the reading itself. The question asks how far it is from the speed at the instant." |
| 0.1 and 0.004 | `mc.calc.error-is-window` | "The error is not the window's length; it is $5h$, the term left over in line 4." |
| First field 0.05 | slip | "That is the error for $h = 0.01$. Here $h = 0.1$." |
| `otherwise` | | "The reading is $20 + 5h$ and the speed at the instant is the limit. Subtract one from the other." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "A window of length $h$ reads $20 + 5h$. What does the instant itself read?" Point: "The secant's slope is the reading; the dashed line's slope is the speed at the instant", highlighting `scene/line:secant` and `scene/handle:P`. Step: "$(20 + 5 \times 0.1) - 20$ for the first window; the same with 0.004 for the second."
- **Why?** (`rep: algebra`) Yours: "A window of length $h$ reads $20 + 5h$, so it overstates the speed by exactly $5h$: 0.5 m/s for 0.1 s and 0.02 m/s for 0.004 s." Then: "An estimate with a known error is worth far more than one without. Lesson 27 builds the same accounting for a computer's estimates, where a second error appears."
- **Solution line:** "Error $= 5h$: $5 \times 0.1 = 0.5$ and $5 \times 0.004 = 0.02$."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "Name the average and instantaneous rates; a window's error is the leftover term." Notes: "Learners who answer with the window's length have confused the window with its error; ask what the 0.1 s window reads and what the instant reads." Reps: `algebra`, `picture`.

---

**Screen 6. Any instant** (`generalise`, id `any-time`)

- **Prose:** "Same steps, any instant $t = a$."
- **Rule** (`rule`, `tex`): $\frac{s(a + h) - s(a)}{h} = \frac{5(a + h)^2 - 5a^2}{h} = 10a + 5h \;\to\; 10a$. The rate at every instant at once is itself a function of time.
- **Widget:** `scene`, graph only (the tower has gone). $P$ is draggable along the curve for $a$ from 0 to 4, with a small fixed $h$; after Check a live readout shows "rate at $a$: $10a$".
- **Question:** "So the ball's speed at time $a$ is $10a$ m/s. What is its speed at $t = 3$, and at what time is it falling at 25 m/s?"
- **Answer input:** two `numeric-input` fields, units "m/s" and "s".
- **Correct:** 30 m/s; 2.5 s. **Reason:** "$10 \times 3 = 30$, and $10a = 25$ at $a = 2.5$."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| First field 45 | `mc.calc.position-as-rate` | "45 m is the distance fallen by $t = 3$." |
| First field 25 | `mc.calc.average-equals-instant` | "25 m/s is the average over the window from 2 to 3 seconds, screen 3's $h = 1$ row. At $t = 3$ itself the speed is $10 \times 3$." |
| Second field 3 | `mc.calc.average-equals-instant` | "The window from 2 to 3 averages 25, but the instant at which the speed is 25 solves $10a = 25$." |
| Second field 2 | slip | "At $t = 2$ the speed is 20." |
| `otherwise` | | "Use the rule: speed $= 10a$." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "Put $a = 3$ into $10a$; then solve $10a = 25$." Point: "Drag this point to the instant you want", highlighting `scene/handle:P`. Step: "$10 \times 3$; and $a = \frac{25}{10}$."
- **Why?** (`rep: algebra`) Yours: "$10 \times 3 = 30$ m/s, and $10a = 25$ at $a = 2.5$ s." Then: "2.5 is the midpoint of the window from 2 to 3, which averaged 25. For this ball every window's average equals the speed at the window's midpoint. Is that true of every motion? The next screen tests it."
- **Solution line:** "$10 \times 3 = 30$; $10a = 25$ gives $a = 2.5$."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "The rate at every instant at once is a function of time, $10a$ for this ball." Notes: "A learner who answers 25 for the first field has reused the window from 2 to 3; ask about the instant $t = 3$ itself." Reps: `algebra`, `picture`.

---

**Screen 7. The centred radar** (`trap`, id `centred`, `trap: { mc: mc.calc.centred-window-exact, bait: A }`)

- **Prose:** "A second radar averages over a window centred on the instant, from $2 - h$ to $2 + h$. For the falling ball it reads exactly 20 m/s, whatever $h$ is. Now it is pointed at a cart whose position after $t$ seconds is $x(t) = t^3$ metres. The cart's speed at $t = 1$ is 3 m/s: screen 4's steps give $3 + 3h + h^2 \to 3$."
- **Widget:** `scene` in centred-window mode: $Q$ at $a + h$ and $Q'$ at $a - h$ (`handle:Qm`), the secant through them, and the readout labelled "centred reading". It opens on the ball ($5t^2$ at $a = 2$, readout 20.000 for every stop) and switches to the cart ($t^3$ at $a = 1$) with the readout hidden until Check; `slider:h` stops at 1, 0.5 and 0.1.
- **Question:** "With $h = 0.1$, what does the centred radar read for the cart at $t = 1$?" (`choice`)

| Option | Correct? | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Exactly 3 m/s: a centred window cancels the error (the bait) | no | `mc.calc.centred-window-exact` | "It reads 3.01. The errors from the two sides cancel only in part: for $t^3$ a term $h^2$ survives. For $5t^2$ there was nothing left to survive." |
| B. 3.01 m/s: closer than a one-sided window, but not exact | yes | | "Yes: $\frac{1.1^3 - 0.9^3}{0.2} = \frac{0.602}{0.2} = 3.01$." |
| C. 3.31 m/s | no | `mc.calc.centred-same-as-one-sided` | "3.31 is the one-sided reading from 1 to 1.1. The centred window runs from 0.9 to 1.1." |
| D. 0 m/s: the two sides cancel completely | no | `mc.calc.instant-is-zero` | "The cart moves forward on both sides of $t = 1$, so the distances add; they do not cancel." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "Compute $1.1^3$ and $0.9^3$." Point: "These two points set the centred reading", highlighting `scene/handle:Q` and `scene/handle:Qm`. Step: "$1.1^3 = 1.331$ and $0.9^3 = 0.729$; divide their difference by the 0.2 s between them."
- **Why?** (`rep: algebra`) Shown: "$\frac{(1 + h)^3 - (1 - h)^3}{2h} = 3 + h^2$, which is 3.01 at $h = 0.1$." Then: "A centred window cancels the part of the error that grows like $h$ and leaves a part that grows like $h^2$: much better, not exact. It looked exact for the ball because a quadratic leaves no $h^2$ part, and lesson 27 builds the central difference on exactly this."
- **Solution line:** "$\frac{1.331 - 0.729}{0.2} = 3.01$."
- **On reveal:** the lesson player plays Bertie's `surprised` one-shot (bertie.md 5.3). A revealed trap screen does not enter the Redo queue (assessment.md 2.2).
- **Tutor.** Focus: "A centred window is better than a one-sided one, but not exact." Notes: "Most learners choose A after watching the ball's centred readings; the screen exists to be failed once. Ask them to compute the two cubes before choosing." Reps: `algebra`, `count`. Avoid: "Taylor", "central difference" (lesson 27 names it).

---

**Screen 8. A rate of zero** (`problem`, id `top`, two parts; the edge case)

- **Prose:** "A ball is thrown straight up. Its height after $t$ seconds is $y(t) = 20t - 5t^2$ metres."
- **Widget:** `scene` with $y(t) = 20t - 5t^2$ on $[0, 4]$, $P$ at $t = 2$, `slider:h` stops at $\pm0.5$, $\pm0.1$ and $\pm0.01$, and a `table` of readings ordered $h = -0.5, -0.1, -0.01, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5$.
- **Part (a) question:** "What is the rate of change of the height at $t = 2$?" (`numeric-input`, unit "m/s")
- **Correct:** 0. **Reason:** "The reading for a window of length $h$ is $-5h$, which goes to 0."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| 20 | `mc.calc.position-as-rate` | "20 m is the height at $t = 2$, the top of the throw." |
| 10 | `mc.calc.average-from-start` | "$20 \div 2 = 10$ is the average rate since the throw." |
| $\pm2.5$, $\pm0.05$ or $\pm5$ | `mc.calc.limit-is-last-value` | "That is the reading for one window (0.5 s, 0.01 s or 1 s). The question asks what the readings approach as the window shrinks." |
| `otherwise` | | "Simplify $\frac{y(2 + h) - y(2)}{h}$ as on screen 4, then let $h \to 0$." |

- **Part (b) question:** "So at $t = 2$ the rate is 0. Is the ball at rest at $t = 2$?" (`choice`)

| Option | Correct? | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|---|
| Yes: a rate of zero means it has stopped | no | `mc.calc.zero-rate-means-stopped` | "At rest would mean not moving over some window of time. Every window round $t = 2$ shows motion: up before, down after." |
| Only at that instant: every window round $t = 2$ shows the ball moving | yes | | "Yes. The rate is 0 at one instant and over no window." |
| No: the rate at the top does not exist, because the ball changes direction | no | `mc.calc.turnaround-no-rate` | "The readings from both sides close in on 0, so the rate exists. 0.2 lesson 109's corner had none because its two sides disagreed." |
| Yes, for as long as the readings round to 0 | no | slip | "Rounding hides small numbers; the ball is still moving." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "Read the windows just before and just after $t = 2$. What sign does each reading have?" Point: "Compare these two readings", highlighting `table/cell:1,reading` and `table/cell:4,reading`. Step: "$y(2 + h) = 20 - 5h^2$, so the reading is $\frac{-5h^2}{h} = -5h$: positive just before the top, negative just after."
- **Why?** (`rep: picture`) Yours: "The reading is $-5h$: $+0.5$ for the window just before the top, $-0.5$ just after, and its limit is 0." Then: "A rate of 0 at an instant is not rest. Every smooth peak has one, which is why optimisation hunts for rates of 0 (lesson 35)."
- **Solution line:** "(a) $\frac{(20 - 5h^2) - 20}{h} = -5h \to 0$. (b) At rest only at that instant."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "A rate of zero at an instant is not rest over any window." Notes: "Learners who say the rate does not exist are recalling 0.2 lesson 109's corner; ask whether the two sides agree here." Reps: `picture`, `algebra`.

---

**Screen 9. Transfer: a contract's delta** (`problem`, id `delta`)

- **Prose:** "Trading desks ask the same question about prices. A contract pays $V(x) = x^2$ pounds when a share trades at $x$ pounds, and the share is at £100. A risk system bumps the share price up by £1, re-prices the contract, and divides the change by the bump."
- **Widget:** `scene` with $V(x) = x^2$ on $[90, 110]$, $P$ at 100, `slider:h` in pounds with stops at $\pm1$, $\pm0.1$ and $\pm0.01$, and a `table` of $h$, $V(100 + h) - V(100)$ and the reading.
- **Question:** "What does the risk system report, and what is the exact rate of change at £100?"
- **Answer input:** two `numeric-input` fields, unit "£ per £".
- **Correct:** 201 and 200. **Reason:** "$\frac{101^2 - 100^2}{1} = 201$; in general the reading is $200 + h$, which goes to 200."

| Wrong answer | Misconception | Feedback line |
|---|---|---|
| Second field 201 | `mc.calc.limit-is-last-value` | "201 is the £1 bump's reading. Shrink the bump: 200.1, then 200.01." |
| Second field 199 | `mc.calc.limit-is-last-value` | "199 is the reading for a £1 fall. Shrink the bump." |
| First field 200 | `mc.calc.average-equals-instant` | "200 is the exact rate. A £1 bump reads $\frac{101^2 - 100^2}{1}$, a little more." |
| 10,000 or 10,201 | `mc.calc.position-as-rate` | "That is a value of the contract, not how fast it changes." |
| Second field 2 | slip | "Close: the rate is $2x$, and here $x = 100$." |
| `otherwise` | | "Same algebra as the ball: $\frac{(100 + h)^2 - 100^2}{h}$. Put $h = 1$ for the system, then let $h \to 0$." |

- **Hints.** Nudge: "Expand $(100 + h)^2 - 100^2$." Point: "The bump is this slider; the reading is this secant's slope", highlighting `scene/slider:h` and `scene/line:secant`. Step: "$\frac{200h + h^2}{h} = 200 + h$: put $h = 1$, then let $h$ shrink."
- **Why?** (`rep: algebra`) Yours: "The bump reads $200 + h$: 201 for £1, and the exact rate is 200. The bump's error is exactly $h$, as the radar's was $5h$." Then: "Traders call this rate the contract's **delta**: about £200 of value for each £1 move in the share. Banks estimate deltas by bumping and re-pricing, and lesson 27 chooses the size of the bump."
- **Solution line:** "$\frac{10{,}201 - 10{,}000}{1} = 201$; $\frac{(100 + h)^2 - 100^2}{h} = 200 + h \to 200$."
- **Tutor.** Focus: "The same limit gives a contract's sensitivity to the share price." Notes: "A learner who reports the bump's reading as the exact rate needs to shrink the bump; ask what a one-penny bump reads." Reps: `algebra`, `picture`.

---

**Screen 10. Summary** (`summary`, id `summary`)

- **Ideas** (exactly three).
  1. A window's average rate of change is $\frac{s(a + h) - s(a)}{h}$: change in output over change in input, the slope of a secant.
  2. The rate at an instant is the number those averages approach as $h \to 0$ from both sides. Find it exactly by simplifying while $h \ne 0$; the term left over is the window's exact error.
  3. Done at every instant, this gives the rate as a function of time ($10a$ for $5t^2$). A rate of 0 at an instant is not rest.
- **Card** (`crd.calc.instantaneous-rate`, saved to the Notebook). Rate of $s$ at $a$ $= \lim_{h \to 0}\frac{s(a + h) - s(a)}{h}$, when both sides agree. For $5t^2$: $10a$, and a window of length $h$ reads $10a + 5h$. For $x^2$: $2x$, and a bump of $h$ reads $2x + h$.
- **Trap.** "A centred window is better, not exact: for $t^3$ at 1 it reads $3 + h^2$."

### 7.3 Lesson quiz (5 items, 1 mark each, difficulty 2 to 3, one attempt each, no hints, Why? after each)

| # | Item | Type | Difficulty | Correct | Diagnosed wrong answers | Why? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | For $f(x) = x^2 + 3x$ and $h \ne 0$, simplify $\frac{f(1 + h) - f(1)}{h}$ so that no $h$ is left in a denominator | X | 2 | $5 + h$ | $3 + h$ (`mc.calc.expand-square-wrong`: $(1 + h)^2$ taken as $1 + h^2$); $2 + h$ (slip: the $3x$ part dropped); $\frac{5h + h^2}{h}$ (form: "divide each term by $h$") | "$f(1 + h) = 4 + 5h + h^2$ and $f(1) = 4$, so the quotient is $\frac{5h + h^2}{h} = 5 + h$." |
| Q2 | Hence the instantaneous rate of change of $f$ at $x = 1$ | N | 2 | 5 | 6 (`mc.calc.limit-is-last-value`: $5 + h$ at $h = 1$); 4 (`mc.calc.position-as-rate`: $f(1)$); 0 (`mc.calc.instant-is-zero`) | "$5 + h \to 5$ as $h \to 0$." |
| Q3 | A radar averages a cart's speed over the window from $t = 3$ to $t = 3.2$ seconds. The cart's position is $5t^2$ metres. By how many m/s does the reading exceed the cart's speed at $t = 3$? | N | 3 | 1 | 31 (`mc.calc.reading-as-error`: the reading itself); 30 (`mc.calc.reading-as-error`: the speed itself); 0.2 (`mc.calc.error-is-window`) | "The reading is $30 + 5h$ with $h = 0.2$, which is 31; the speed at $t = 3$ is 30, so the excess is $5 \times 0.2 = 1$ m/s." |
| Q4 | A window centred on the instant, from $a - h$ to $a + h$, gives exactly the rate at $a$ for every $h$. For which position function? | C | 3 | $x(t) = 3t^2 + t$ | "$x(t) = t^3$" (`mc.calc.centred-window-exact`: "it reads $3a^2 + h^2$"); "$x(t) = \sqrt t$" ("a centred window errs for $\sqrt t$ too"); "all of them, because the two sides' errors cancel" (`mc.calc.centred-window-exact`) | "For any quadratic $pt^2 + qt + c$ the centred reading is $\frac{p\left((a + h)^2 - (a - h)^2\right) + 2qh}{2h} = 2pa + q$, with no $h$ left." |
| Q5 | The position is $s(t) = 5t^2 + 2t$ metres. Write its rate of change at time $a$ as an expression in $a$ | X | 3 | $10a + 2$ | $10a$ (slip: the $2t$ part dropped); $5a^2 + 2a$ (`mc.calc.position-as-rate`); $10a + 2 + 5h$ (`mc.calc.limit-is-last-value`: "the window is still open: let $h \to 0$") | "$\frac{s(a + h) - s(a)}{h} = 10a + 2 + 5h \to 10a + 2$." |

Single and multiple choice: 1 of 5 items (20%). The quiz creates the FSRS cards for the four introduced skills (assessment.md 5.3). Mastered needs 75% of the marks on the first completion (assessment.md decision 26), which on five one-mark items means 4 of 5; any wrong item joins the Redo queue as a fresh generated variant at the same level (`gen.calc.difference-quotient-simplify`, `gen.calc.window-error`, `gen.calc.rate-function-polynomial`, `gen.calc.centred-window`).
